| 354 | **Gemini CLI publishes nothing an external process can read: no pid file, no lock, no open fd, no environment variable** | `gemini` 0.56.0 under a pty in a throwaway `HOME`, `/proc/<pid>/fd` and `/proc/locks` sampled every 60 ms — 643 samples on the re-exec'd child | **0 regular-file fds and 0 lock-table entries in all 643 samples**, and no `GEMINI_*` variable naming a session. The session file *is* created at boot — `~/.gemini/tmp/<project>/chats/session-<ISO>-<8hex>.jsonl`, whose `projectHash` is verified to be sha256 of the project root path — but nothing binds it to a process, so cwd and mtime are the only handle, which is the time-bound-not-identity failure of [#260](#). Gemini 0.56.0 also **could not authenticate on this machine at all** (*"This client is no longer supported for Gemini Code Assist for individuals"*), so nothing about a running gemini turn was measured |
| 355 | **Herdr's agent detection is two stages, and [#75](#) describes only the second: the *label* is the foreground process name with no verification, and the *state* comes from versioned per-agent manifests** | copied `/usr/bin/cat` to a file named `claude` and ran it in a throwaway headless pane; then printed a perfect Claude permission prompt into a plain `bash` pane; then `herdr agent explain --json` and `herdr server agent-manifests --json` | The renamed `cat` reported `agent: "claude"` and was served the real Claude manifest. The perfect prompt under `bash` produced **no `agent` at all** — screen text never *creates* a label. Killing the process clears `agent` to `null` and `agent_status` to `unknown`. The state comes from manifests with three sources per agent (bundled, remote from `herdr.dev`, and a local override), highest version wins; the claude manifest carries 16 rules over seven regions (`osc_title`, `osc_progress`, `bottom_non_empty_lines(N)`, `whole_recent`, `prompt_box_body`, `after_last_horizontal_rule`, `last_non_empty_above_prompt_box`) matched **strict highest-priority-wins**. With a known agent and **no rule matching, herdr publishes `idle`** with `fallback_reason: "default_known_agent_idle_fallback"` — so `idle` from herdr is never evidence a harness is waiting, only that nothing matched |
| 356 | **Herdr does not flap: contrary evidence must persist between 200 ms and 400 ms to move the published state, and burst output does not move it at all** | three 16 s runs against a driven pane polled at 113 Hz, tracing `agent.explain` alongside `pane.get`; then a contrary window varied against a working baseline | Constant working title plus `seq 1 20000`: **2** published transitions. Plain title plus the same burst: **2**. An OSC title alternating working/non-working **200× at 20 Hz**: **4** — and the trace shows the raw `explain` verdict oscillating every ~40 ms throughout while the published `agent_status` stayed pinned at `working`. Contrary windows of 50/100/200 ms produced **zero** non-working episodes; 400/800/1600 ms produced them. Latency of a real change to the snapshot is **under 0.4 s** (median ~0.2 s). So "detection blinking" is not flapping and must not be explained as flapping |
| 357 | **Herdr's `done` means "finished while nobody was looking", it outranks `working` in its own rollup, and only `pane.focus` clears it — no read does** | drove one pane `working`→`idle` focused and unfocused; re-armed `done` and called each read op in turn; drove two panes in one tab through every state pair reading `tab.get` | Focused, `working`→`idle` gives `idle`; **unfocused it gives `done`**, and `pane.focus` collapses it back. `pane.read` (visible and recent), `agent.read --source detection`, `pane.list`, `agent.explain` and `agent.get` **all leave it standing**. Herdr synthesises `done` even on top of a *report*: reporting `idle` on an unfocused pane that was `working` publishes `done`, a state `pane.report_agent` is not allowed to send. Tab rollup gives the total order **`blocked > done > working > idle > unknown`**, which is what `kampr-tui`'s `sidebar::rank` and the Kotlin `statusRank` now both use. Reads are safe; **focus is the one op that destroys the operator's unread marker** |
| 358 | **A herdr report never overrides a matching rule, never expires, and invents an agent on a pane that has none** | reported `working` from three different `source` ids against a pane whose screen matched a live rule; then against a blank screen; then polled a reported pane once a second for 406 s | Precedence is **matching rule > report > idle fallback**. `herdr:claude`, `herdr:mastracode` and `kampr` all lost identically to a live rule. Against the fallback the report wins, and `agent.explain` still shows only the *screen* verdict, so `explain.state` and the published `agent_status` legitimately disagree — explain is not an oracle for what a client sees. **No TTL**: `fakeagent`/`blocked` on a plain `bash` pane held 400/400 samples over 406 s with `revision` unmoved. Of herdr's 17 integrations, **only mastracode, opencode, kilo and kimi report state at all** — the other 13, including **claude and codex**, call `pane.report_agent_session` only, so Claude's lifecycle state is screen-scraped even with herdr's own integration installed |
| 359 | **Herdr cannot rename a session, and the gap is structural: it renames all four things *inside* a session and nothing renames the session itself** | `herdr session` and `--help` on each subcommand; `herdr api schema --json` enumerated for method constants; `strings` on the binary with positive controls | `herdr session` offers exactly `list`, `attach`, `stop`, `delete`. The socket API carries `agent.rename`, `pane.rename`, `tab.rename` and `workspace.rename` and its only session-scoped method is `session.snapshot`; `session.rename` appears **zero** times in the binary while `workspace.rename` appears throughout, so the negative is load-bearing. A session's name is its **directory name and nothing else** — `session.json` contains zero occurrences of it. Moving that directory under a **live** server looks successful on every surface and is a corruption: the server re-opens its state file by path, recreates the old directory and writes there, leaving a ghost session holding the real state. Renaming while stopped is clean and preserves the layout, but every pane becomes a fresh PTY, so everything running in it dies. **What the operator sees renamed in herdr's sidebar is a *workspace*, which Kampr's `rename` op already covers** |
| 360 | **Nothing a child does to a pane's foreground drops its agent label — but a title written *before* the label attaches is lost for good, and herdr then publishes `idle` about a pane whose title says working** | a copy of `/usr/bin/python3` named `claude` (label confirmed) in a throwaway session: it takes its own process group and the tty, then spawns a `sleep` child two ways — same group, and its own group made foreground via `tcsetpgrp` — at 0.5/1/2/3/4/6/10/20 s, `pane.get` and `pane.process_info` sampled together at 25 Hz (4134 samples); then the operator's two **live** claude panes watched read-only at 150 ms for 300 s across a deliberate 45 s Bash tool child; then the first OSC title delayed 0.0–5.0 s past `exec`, read back through `agent.explain`'s `osc_title` `region_preview`/`region_bytes` beside `pane.terminal_title`. `research/probe/label-drop/` | **The label never drops** — `agent: "claude"` through every duration in both arrangements, including 20 s with `foreground_processes: ['sleep']` and claude outside the foreground group entirely. On the live panes, **1991 samples, zero transitions**: a real Claude Bash tool child is spawned into **its own process group and never made the tty's foreground**, so `foreground_processes` reads `['claude']` throughout. The other half is real: **`pane.terminal_title` and the `osc_title` detection region are different state, and the region only records titles written *after* the label attached.** A title written **≤0.15 s** past `exec` is lost (2/2; 1/2 at 0.25 s; adopted 4/4 at ≥0.30 s — herdr's own 100/300 ms detection tick) and is **never backfilled**: 40 s of `region_bytes: 0`, `fallback_reason: default_known_agent_idle_fallback` and a published **`idle`** while `terminal_title` read `◐ probe working`. Re-writing the **byte-identical** title fixes it in ≤0.5 s. The 3-second figure belongs elsewhere: herdr holds `unknown` for **3.33 s** (n=6, zero variance, anchored on the label appearing) before the idle fallback publishes, against ~0.35 s when a rule matches — and no constant backs it, the unstripped binary's 19 633 symbols carry no 3.0/3.2/3.3/3.5 s value in any encoding, only 100/300/500 ms in `spawn_basic_detection_task` and 700/800 ms in `decide_screen_detection_publish`. **The trap that produces the opposite reading**: `bash -c 'sleep 6'` under a `bash` copy named `claude` is **exec-replaced** by its child — the agent process never exists and `foreground_processes` goes `['bash'] → ['sleep']`; `bash -c 'sleep 6; :'` keeps it alive and the label holds for the full 6 s. Supersedes the provisional reading in `docs/14-agent-status.md` §2; **amends [#355](#)** — the label is the process name, and the screen drives only the state |
| 361 | **The conversation pump's warm latency is nothing like the 15 s constant it reads as: a first page costs 2 ms and a new turn a mean 200 ms, set by `POLL` and the page read alone** | a synthetic Claude session in a throwaway herdr session — a `bash` copy `exec`ed as `claude` so `pane.process_info` reports the right foreground process, plus `pane.report_agent`, plus a hand-written `.jsonl` under a tempdir `journals.home`. `pane.watch {conversation: true}` timed to the first `convo` frame, 8 runs at 46 turns and 6 at 4000 turns (9.3 MB); then a turn appended at 16 phases swept across the poll period | First page **1.7 / 2.0 / 3.4 ms** (min/median/max) at 46 turns, **115.3 / 116.5 / 150.5 ms** at 9.3 MB. The pump enters its loop with `due = true`, so resolve + page + drain all run before any tick is awaited — **neither `POLL` nor `RESOLVE_EVERY` is on the first-page path**, and the large figure is the fold's first read, matching the 154 ms / 29.4 MB already cited in `convo.rs`. The tail is a textbook sawtooth, `latency ≈ 400 − (write phase mod 400)`: min **10.7 ms**, median **209.4 ms**, max **397.7 ms** — `POLL` and nothing else. Measures the node from file write to wire; it does **not** include how long a real harness takes to flush a record, which is upstream of all of it and unmeasured |
| 362 | **The cold-start retry ladder is non-monotonic — a transcript that appears at 7 s waits *longer* than one that appears at 11 s** | the same harness with the watch sent before any transcript existed, the transcript then written at 0.3 / 1 / 3 / 5 / 7 / 11 s and the first `convo` frame timed; two full runs | 0.3 s→**2.00 s**, 1 s→2.00, 3 s→4.00, 5 s→6.00, **7 s→15.00**, **11 s→15.00**. `RETRY_EVERY = 2s` with `FAST_RETRIES = 5` reads as a 10 s fast window and measures as **6 s**: at t≈0 the initial `due` resolve *and* `retry`'s immediate first tick each count a miss, so the ladder is 0, 0, 2, 4, 6 and goes quiet at five. Anything appearing after that waits for `RESOLVE_EVERY`, which fires at exactly **15.0 s** (`recheck`'s immediate tick is consumed before the loop, `retry`'s is not). **A transcript materialising at 7 s therefore costs 8 s of dead air, worse than one at 11 s.** [#311](#) records a 2 min 42 s gap between a session opening and a transcript existing, which lands squarely in the 15 s regime. Reproducible to the millisecond across both runs |
| 363 | **The conversation pane that "stops updating" is the queued-prompt facet burying it: 909 of 992 `enqueue` records are `<task-notification>` envelopes, and a `dequeue` carries no `content` so the fold can only pop the head by position** | replayed the queue fold over every transcript in `~/.claude/projects` (992 `enqueue`, of which 909 begin `<task-notification>` and 83 are operator prose); dumped the raw shape of each of the four operations; then replayed one live session to the exact second of a reported screenshot | `enqueue` carries `content`; `remove` carries `content` **and** a `reason` (`absorbed_mid_turn`); **`dequeue` carries neither** — only a timestamp; `popAll` carries the *next* operator message, which is why the queue looks correct again the moment a person types. So an `enqueue` with no matching `remove` sticks at position 0 and **every later `dequeue` pops the wrong entry**, draining one short for ever. At the screenshot's 20:40:58 the fold left **3** standing — a 16:41 and an 18:07 task-notification and a 20:02 question answered 38 minutes earlier — matching the three blocks on screen exactly. The client files queued prompts *after every record* on a view pinned to its end, so those three hid the whole conversation and the pane read as though it had stopped updating, with the harness's own plumbing attributed to the operator. Reconciling against the user record the harness writes when it takes a prompt, and refusing envelopes as prompts, takes the machine from **5 phantom entries to 0** and that session from 3 to 0 |

## What the attachment path does between a browser and a pane (ChromeHeadless 151.0.0.0 under Karma, `:shared:wasmJsBrowserTest`; a real node and a real herdr for the socket half)

The operator, at 0.1.38: *"wasm when i attach files they don't seem to actually end up in the
message"*, and *"i cant paste files/images from clipboard need to save and attach"*. The first is a
size, not a platform; the second is a gesture that had no seam at all.

| # | Reading | How | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 364 | **The node's client socket refused every attachment over ~768 KiB by killing the connection, while the client had already said "sent"** | `a_paste_the_size_of_a_photograph_crosses_the_socket_rather_than_closing_it` — a real node, a real herdr pane, a 2 MiB `paste` frame | `send: Io(ConnectionReset)` **mid-write**, before the body was out. `MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES` was `1024 * 1024` against a comment reading *"the wire protocol's biggest legitimate one is a `prefs` blob, capped at 2 KiB"* — written before `paste` existed, and `paste` is the one message that is legitimately large. The client checks 8 MiB (`MOST_BYTES_HANDED_OVER`) and **says so on screen**, so everything in the 0.75–8 MiB band was accepted, base64'd, sent, and dropped on the floor. Nothing reports it: a paste has no acknowledgement, `handoverOf` returns `Sent` the instant `io.send` returns, and the socket's own death is indistinguishable from any other reconnect. The ceiling is derived from `attach::MAX_BYTES` now rather than chosen, so the two cannot drift again. Every platform was affected identically — a phone photograph is the same size as a desktop screenshot — and the browser is simply where the operator noticed |
| 365 | **A synthetic `ClipboardEvent` carrying a `DataTransfer` is enough to drive the whole paste path in a headless browser, which has no clipboard of its own** | `BrowserPasteTest`, three tests: `new DataTransfer()` + `data.items.add(new File(...))` + `new ClipboardEvent('paste', {clipboardData: data, bubbles: true})` dispatched on `document.body` | All three constructible in Chromium 151, and a capture-phase `window` listener sees an event that started on the body. So the seam is testable without a clipboard, a permission, or a real screenshot. `event.defaultPrevented` reads back from the dispatcher, which is how the *other* half is asserted: a clipboard carrying only `text/plain` comes back **`false`** and stays Compose's to insert, and one carrying a file comes back **`true`** — a file copied in a file manager also puts its name on the clipboard as text, and pasting the name into the reply box is not what was asked for |
| 366 | **Nobody is waiting for most of a paste's life, so the queue behind the listener is load-bearing and the obvious test does not reach it** | the same suite. Two files in one clipboard are two independent `FileReader`s; the first test asked for the second file immediately after the first | Deleting `box.ready.push(value)` — the branch that keeps a file nobody has asked for — left **all three tests green**, because the second read finished *after* the next `pastedFile()` had already parked a waiter. A 200 ms real-time settle between them (`setTimeout` through a promise; `runTest`'s virtual clock cannot advance a browser's `FileReader`) makes the file land with no waiter, and the mutation then fails exactly one test. The listener itself is installed once for the life of the page and is deliberately **not** disposed with the composable — a listener that comes and goes with a pane is absent for the paste that arrives during a view switch; what is scoped to the composable is the waiter |
| 367 | **~~Unmeasured: whether a `paste` event reaches the page when Compose's focus is on the `CANVAS` rather than on its offscreen editable~~** — measured and closed by [#370](#): it does, on a bare canvas and on one inside a shadow root alike | — | Both pane surfaces put the focus on an editable element in the flows that matter ([#308](#)), and an editable target certainly fires. A non-editable focused element fires `paste` in Chromium and historically did not in Firefox, and neither is measured here — the harness dispatches its own events, so it cannot answer what a browser dispatches on its own. Worth a real-browser run if a paste is ever reported as ignored while a sheet is open |

## Where a pasted file can land, on the two platforms that ship (ChromeHeadless 151 under CDP; `Medium_Phone_API_35` emulator, Android 15; Compose Multiplatform 1.11.1 / androidx Compose 1.12.0 read off the compiled artifacts)

The operator, on 0.1.38: *"i cant paste files/images from clipboard need to save and attach — could
we trick it so it acts exactly like pasting in claude itself"*, and then *"lets measure"* about the
one thing [#367](#) had left open.

| # | Reading | How | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 368 | **Every route a pasted image takes on Android ends at a `TextFieldState` field, and the composer was not one** — so a `contentReceiver` on it would have been dead code | `javap`/`strings` over `foundation-android-1.12.0` and `ui-android-1.12.0`: who reads `ReceiveContentConfiguration`, and what `RecordingInputConnection.commitContent` returns | Only `androidx.compose.foundation.text.input.internal.*` reads the configuration — `TextFieldDecoratorModifierNode`, `AndroidTextInputSession_androidKt`, `TextFieldSelectionState` — which is the **new** `BasicTextField(TextFieldState)` and nothing else. The legacy `BasicTextField(TextFieldValue, onValueChange)` reaches none of them, its `RecordingInputConnection.commitContent` **returns `false` on both paths** (`iconst_0; ireturn`, twice), and nothing in `compose-ui` sets `EditorInfo.contentMimeTypes` on it — so Gboard cannot even offer image insert there. All three Android paste routes (keyboard `commitContent`, the long-press paste menu, drag-and-drop) are therefore the same seam, and the composer had to migrate to `TextFieldState` before any of them could work. That migration cost two regressions worth naming: `snapshotFlow`/`SideEffect` report a draft **a frame late**, and the frame they are late by is the one that takes the composer out of the composition — so the report moved into an `InputTransformation`, where the old `onValueChange` was; and the new field publishes a **scroll action of its own**, so `onNode(hasScrollAction())` stopped naming the transcript |
| 369 | **A real clipboard image pasted into the reply box arrives as bytes, and nothing of it is left for the text field to insert** | `PastingAFileTest` on the emulator: a URI on the real system clipboard, `SemanticsActions.PasteText` on the real composer, `contentReceiver` on the column above it | Byte-for-byte match at the far end, and the field is left **empty** — the item is consumed, so no `content://` line lands in somebody's reply. Two details worth writing down. `ContentResolver.getType` answers **null** for an `android.resource://` URI where it answers `image/png` for a gallery's `content://` one, and the display-name query answers null too — **neither is on the path**, because `ClientMsg.Paste` carries no media type at all and the node sniffs the extension off the body (`paste::write`). And the item has to be claimed *before* its bytes are read: `consume` wants an answer while the paste is still happening, and the URI is a permission grant that dies with it. Mutation: replacing `acceptsPastedFiles` with the identity modifier fails with *"the paste never reached the receiver"* |
| 370 | **Chromium dispatches a real `paste` to whatever has the focus, editable or not — and a canvas inside a shadow root is no exception.** [#367](#) is closed | `research/probe/browser-paste/`: real Chromium 151 over CDP, a real system clipboard seeded through `navigator.clipboard`, a real ctrl+V by `Input.dispatchKeyEvent`, against a contenteditable, a bare `canvas[tabindex=0]`, and a `canvas[tabindex=0]` inside an open shadow root | All three fire at a **capture-phase `window`** listener — the exact shape Kampr installs — with `composed: true` and `eventPhase: 1`. The shadow one is retargeted to the host (`target: host`, `activeElement: host/CANVAS`), which is the spec's behaviour and means the boundary Compose renders behind ([#307](#)) costs nothing. So a paste reaches Kampr wherever Compose has put the focus, sheet open or not. Focus emulation is required for any of it: a headless page is not "focused" and the async clipboard API refuses an unfocused document |
| 371 | **Headless Chromium has no image clipboard, by two independent routes** | the same harness: `navigator.clipboard.write` with an `image/png` `ClipboardItem`, and `document.execCommand('copy')` over a selected `<img>` | `DataError: Failed to read or decode ClipboardItemData for type image/png` from the async API — the PNG is valid, and decodes fine outside the browser — and a flat `refused` from the copy command. So the payload half of a *real* chord cannot be measured here, only the dispatch half. It does not matter: the browser decides the target from the focus before it looks at the clipboard ([#370](#)), and the payload is what [#365](#) drives with synthetic events. Same shape as [#276](#) — the harness cannot be the thing, so measure the half that separates |
| 372 | **A path in the operator's own turn is the one path a node can resolve without guessing at English** | `crates/kampr-node/tests/pasted.rs`, five cases against a real Claude transcript and a real pastes directory | `filePathOf` on the client refuses to look for paths in prose, and it is right to: a guess that offers to fetch a file is worse than not offering one. The node's advantage is not that it reads English better — it is that **it wrote these files**, into a directory it owns, minutes ago. Three facts and no readings: the token is absolute, its parent is *this node's* `pastes/`, and the file is still on disk. The third is load-bearing on its own — the sweep is a day away and a turn is for ever, so a turn from last week must show the path it always did rather than offer a picture that 404s. Each of the three mutations (containment, existence, `Role::User`) kills exactly one test |

## What herdr's own client actually draws, and how much of the screen the pane gets

The operator, on 0.1.41: *"the intention here was this to almost 'feel like' herdr just with kampr
functionality, we drifted very far from what that was supposed to be"*, and *"basically look at how
herdr renders all of this and pull us much closer to that"*. Measured against a real `herdr 0.8.2`
in a throwaway `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, a TUI client forked under a 100x30 PTY, rendered off the client's
own bytes with `research/probe/vt.py`. `HERDR_ENV` and its four companions have to be cleared in the
child or the client refuses to start — this repo's agents run *inside* a pane.

| # | Reading | How | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 373 | **herdr reserves no row at the bottom: at a 100x30 client the pane's own `tput lines` is 29 — every row below the tab strip — and the shell prompt sits on the last one** | `herdr --session rowsprobe` under a 100x30 PTY, sidebar collapsed with `prefix b`, then `clear; seq 1 60` and `clear; tput lines; tput cols` read back off the client's screen | Row 0 is the tab strip (`   1     +`); rows **1–29** are the pane, and `seq`'s `60` lands on row 28 with the prompt on **29**. `tput cols` is **95** = 100 − 3 (the collapsed sidebar rail) − 1 (the `│` separator) − 1 (the scrollbar column, [#230](#)'s finding in herdr's own hand — `▕`/`▐` paint the right-most column). So there is **no status line and no hint bar**: the whole of the client below the strip is the pane. Kampr at the same size spends `[strip 1][panes][status 1][footer 1]` plus a 2-row border and hands the PTY **25** rows — four fewer, 14% of the screen, to chrome herdr does not draw |
| 374 | **herdr's modal footers overlay the pane's last row rather than occupying a row of their own, and in the pane keymap there is no footer at all** | the same client: `\x02` held, then `\x02[`, then `\x02r`, with the screen rendered after each | Steady state, row 29 is pane content. Press the prefix and row 29 reads `PREFIX  esc cancel  ctrl+b send prefix  w workspace nav  ? keybinds`; `prefix [` replaces it with COPY's binds and `prefix r` with RESIZE's — the three footers [#290](#) recorded, now placed. `esc` puts the pane's own row back. **Nothing is drawn there in `Mode::Pane`**, which is the whole difference from kampr's permanent `^b ? help  ^b w sidebar  ^b ⇧h herd  ^b q detach`: herdr's hints cost a row only while a mode is open, and the row they cost is borrowed, not reserved |
| 375 | **A single unsplit pane in herdr has no border at all; the box only appears once there are two panes to separate** | the same client: one pane, then `prefix v`, then `prefix -` | One pane is flush — content starts at column 4 (past the collapsed rail and its separator) on row 1, with no `┌─┐`. After `prefix v` each pane is a full `Borders::ALL` box whose bottom edge is on row 29, and after `prefix -` the horizontal split puts one box's `└` on row 15 and the next box's `┌` on row 16 — the boxes tile with no gutter between them. Kampr draws its box unconditionally, so a lone pane pays 2 rows and 2 columns for a border with nothing on the other side of it. The identity that border's title carries is already in the tab strip, which is where herdr leaves it |
| 376 | **herdr grabs the mouse exactly as kampr does, so the host terminal's own select-and-copy is gone in both and the difference the operator sees is not the grab** | the private modes the client sets at startup, read off its first 3 s of output | `1049` (alt screen), then `1000`, `1002`, `1003`, `1015`, `1006` **set** — button, drag *and* any-motion reporting in SGR — plus `2004` bracketed paste, `1004` focus events, `2031` colour-scheme updates. Identical in kind to kampr's single `EnableMouseCapture`. So "copying pulls in the TUI's own chrome" is not a mouse-grab difference: it is that a host-terminal shift-drag crosses whatever kampr has put on those rows, and kampr puts a sidebar, a status line and a hint bar there where herdr puts pane. Kampr's *own* drag-select was already clamped to one pane (`mouse.rs`, `placed.pane == drag.pane` on both extend and finish) and is not implicated |

## Three reports off one phone, and where each of them actually was

The operator, on 0.1.42: *"app wise text highlights weird sometimes when backgrounding and
foregrounding"*, *"the paste from terminal when I long press on side of screen draws off screen"*,
and *"the terminals hidden input field doesn't seem to keep up to date with the rendered text,
backspacing deletes from terminal but keyboard autocomplete keeps the mistyped text"* — with a
screenshot of a pane wearing a red band across three rows that nothing in the frame had asked for.

| # | Reading | How | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 377 | **Pen ids are promised only for the life of a connection, and the client kept the connection before's table across the reconnect a backgrounded phone makes** | `StylePaletteTest` driving a real `KamprStore` through two greetings; `StyleSyncTest` driving `StyleTable` and `ResolvedStyles` | The node interns per socket (`Wire`: *"its own style table, its own queue"*), so connection two's id 3 is whatever pen **it** met third. `StyleTable.reset()` existed and **nothing called it** — dead code, and the missing call is the defect — so a reconnect wrote its own pens over the front of the old table and left the tail standing. Compounding it exactly: `ResolvedStyles.sync` re-resolved on a **size change** only, and a reconnect that meets *fewer* pens than the last one leaves the size where it was, so every cell on screen went on being painted in the colours of a socket that had closed. That is a red ground on rows that never had one, arriving on the return to the foreground and at no other time. Both halves mutation-tested: restoring either alone leaves one test red |
| 378 | **The selection pill was clamped at the left margin and at no other edge, so a long press in the right-hand third hung 182 dp of it off a 411 dp phone** | `PastingIntoAPaneTest.aPillRaisedAtTheEdgeOfTheScreenIsStillOnTheScreen`: the real `TerminalView` at 411x914, the report's own gesture at 97% across, `getUnclippedBoundsInRoot` on the last button | Its right edge measured **593 dp**. Copy stayed on screen and **Paste and the mode toggle did not** — the affordance a long press exists to offer, unreachable exactly where a right thumb lands ([#369](#) is what put Paste there in the first place). The placement modifier laid out at an absolute pixel with no reference to the box it was in; the same arithmetic holds the drag handles now, since a handle that cannot be touched cannot be dragged |
| 379 | **The hidden field's buffer only ever grew: a backspace this app consumes takes a character off the pane and leaves the editor holding it** | `FieldTextInputTest`, the real `FieldTextInput` under `runComposeUiTest`: type `helo`, press Backspace, read `SemanticsProperties.EditableText` back | One `\x7f` on the wire, correct — and the editor still reads `…helo`. Every route that is not an IME commit is the same shape: the key row writes straight to the pane through `InputSink.press`, a paste and a held submit likewise, and the buffer heard about none of them. **Measured here:** the divergence, and that it is permanent until the pad floor happens to trip. **Not measured, and no device was attached to measure it on:** that Gboard's suggestion strip is what reads the field back — that is Android's input contract rather than a reading, and it is the operator's report (*"keyboard autocomplete keeps the mistyped text"*) that supplies the symptom. The buffer follows the line now: one character off for a backspace, back to bare padding for anything that ends the line or moves the caret off it, both written through `state.edit` so nothing of it is ever typed at the pane. Three mutations, three tests, one each |
| 381 | **The sideways caret chase was gated on scroll zero, so on any pane that overflows both axes the caret sat off the right edge and no frame could bring it back** | `CaretVisibilityTest.theCaretStaysOnScreenSidewaysWhenTheGridOverflowsBothAxes`: the real `TerminalView` at 411x914, a 94x40 pane at a stored zoom of 1.2, keyboard up, caret typed out to column 90 | `panX` stayed at **0** with `minPanX` at **-470**, putting the caret at **x = 844 on a 411 dp window** — two screen widths off, and typing further right moved it further off. The gate asked `scrollY <= 0.5`, which is the bottom of the *surface*: it stopped being where a follower rests at #175 and stopped being it again with the caret band (#380), so the whole horizontal half of follow-cursor was dead for the ordinary phone case. It asks `following` now, the same question the vertical half asks. `TerminalGeometry.pinned` had no other caller and is gone |

The operator, on 0.1.43: *"when running commands that output some lines and update in place like a
docker pull && docker up -d the wasm app (and maybe others) scroll up/don't track the output"*, and
*"when done sometimes i need to scroll up and back down for it to show me the completed terminal
text entry like it needs to repage or something"*.

| # | Reading | How | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 380 | **The surface rested exactly on the caret floor, so every frame that moved the caret moved the viewport by the whole distance — and an in-place redraw moves it to the top of its block and back several times a second** | `FollowingTheOutputTest`, the real `TerminalView` at 411x914 against a 94x40 pane: an eight-line block drawn top to bottom, then redrawn with the caret sent to the top of it and returned, the way `docker compose pull` refreshes | The block is drawn at rows 2–9 and the caret's round trip moved the surface **132 px, seven rows, each way**, once per refresh. Worse when it settled: walking the caret *down* the block as it drew pulled the surface down with it to `scrollY = 0`, leaving **seven of the eight lines above the fold** the moment the block was complete — which is the scroll up and back that made the finished output appear. The floor is a minimum and never was a resting place; the surface rests in a *band* now — `[caret at the top of the rectangle, caret at the bottom]` — and moves only when the caret leaves it. **Two further readings fell out of it.** `following` could no longer be inferred from the position, because a follower legitimately rests off the floor: `carryHistory` read that as "parked in history" and carried a follower off its own output by everything the pane wrote, #175 by a second route. And the opening scroll is taken twice — the first composition lays out at the placeholder 13sp and the font re-probe corrects it a few frames later, on a 90-row pane from **18 px a row to 10** — so a scroll of 922 px measured in a cell that never existed used to be discarded by the snap to the floor, and under a band clamps to the top of history instead. Three mutations, three tests, one each |
| 381 | **The sideways caret chase was gated on scroll zero, so on any pane that overflows both axes the caret sat off the right edge and no frame could bring it back** | `CaretVisibilityTest.theCaretStaysOnScreenSidewaysWhenTheGridOverflowsBothAxes`: the real `TerminalView` at 411x914, a 94x40 pane at a stored zoom of 1.2, keyboard up, caret typed out to column 90 | `panX` stayed at **0** with `minPanX` at **-470**, putting the caret at **x = 844 on a 411 dp window** — two screen widths off, and typing further right moved it further off. The gate asked `scrollY <= 0.5`, which is the bottom of the *surface*: it stopped being where a follower rests at #175 and stopped being it again with the caret band (#380), so the whole horizontal half of follow-cursor was dead for the ordinary phone case. It asks `following` now, the same question the vertical half asks. `TerminalGeometry.pinned` had no other caller and is gone |

The operator, on 0.1.44: *"can we make selecting text on wasm desktop easier? need to long press
which is a weird concept for desktop"*.

| # | Reading | How | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 382 | **A real mouse on the web build reaches Compose as `PointerType.Mouse`** — so a pointer-type branch is live on wasm, not dead code there | `research/probe/web-pointer/probe.js`: the real `wasmJsBrowserDistribution` served over http, a throwaway `?probe=pointer` page whose `pointerInput` reports every change, driven by CDP `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` in Chrome 151 headless | Every change of the drag reads `type=Mouse`, press to release. `LocalInputModeManager` is a *separate* reading and not a proxy for it: it starts `Keyboard` and flips to `Touch` two events into a **mouse** gesture, so anything gated on `isInTouchMode` on this platform is gated on something that is not the pointer. **The touch half of this probe is the harness, not the platform**: `dispatchMouseEvent` with `pointerType: touch` also arrives as `PointerType.Mouse`, so nothing here measures a real finger in a browser |
| 383 | **A plain mouse drag already selects text in an ordinary `SelectionContainer` on the web build — the long press the report named was the terminal grid's own gesture, not Compose's** | `research/probe/web-pointer/select.js`: a throwaway `?probe=select` page — one line inside a `SelectionContainer` with `LocalTextSelectionColors` forced to `#FF00FF` — dragged by CDP, `Page.captureScreenshot` counted the way `SelectionTest` counts on the JVM | **0** wash pixels before, **10,841** after a straight press-move-release, all of them on rows 24–49, which is the row the glyphs are on. A press held still for 900 ms and then dragged washes **4,593**. So the transcript, the herd strips and every other `SelectionContainer` take a desk's drag as they stand; `terminalGestures` was the one surface that gave the drag to the pan and the selection to a 500 ms hold ([#139](#) is why that hold is measured by travel rather than by time) |

The operator, on 0.1.44: *"claude asked me a question, kampr told me it was blocked and had a
question, listed 3 options but they didn't match the actual options"*, with a screenshot of the
pane holding at a five-option dialog under a three-chip strip that named none of them.

| # | Reading | How | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 384 | **`pending::detect` took the *first* run of `1.` on the visible screen, so any numbered list the agent had written earlier and left standing above its own dialog answered the question instead of the dialog** | The operator's screenshot transcribed into `pending::tests::the_dialog_at_the_foot_of_the_screen_beats_a_numbered_list_left_standing_above_it` — a three-item plan above a five-option `AskUserQuestion` | The detector published the plan's three lines as the options and **`● Right — three arms, then:`** as the question, under the real question's blocked flag: three buttons an operator could press that answered nothing that had been asked. A waiting pane has the thing it is waiting on *last*, so the run is now taken from the bottom of the screen up, and a run with no question above it is skipped rather than ending the search |
| 385 | **The same menu is cut short by its own descriptions on any pane narrow enough to wrap one** — and a menu cut to one option is not a menu, so the screen falls back to whatever list is above it | The same dialog re-wrapped at 80 columns, `pending::tests::an_option_whose_description_wraps_does_not_cut_the_menu_short`, with the run rule reverted to prove it | Options carry a description under them, so at 80 columns they sit **three and four rows apart** — past the fixed two-row gap the run allowed, which stopped the menu at option 1. The gap was never a line count: what separates a menu's options from two unrelated numbered lines is a **blank** line, and two of those mean the list ended. `a_numbered_list_far_from_a_question_is_not_a_prompt`'s three blank lines are still rejected on that rule |
| 386 | **A question the harness wrapped was published as whichever row the question mark landed on** — so the operator was asked `you want me to get the card?` and told nothing about the card | The same 80-column screen, `pending::tests::an_option_whose_description_wraps_does_not_cut_the_menu_short`, with the join reverted to prove it; and `the_trust_prompt_publishes_the_question_and_not_the_link_label` unchanged, whose own `?` falls on the *first* row of its wrap | `question_above` cut at the `?` and took no more, which is right when the mark is on the first row and useless when it is on the third. The rows are now joined backwards from the mark, stopping at a blank line, at the rule that opens the dialog, or at a row that **ends a sentence** — a hard wrap breaks between words, so `All 5 passed.` above a question is output rather than the head of it. The speaker glyph goes too: `●` opens every message Claude writes and `❯` marks the row under its cursor, and both were being published as the first characters of the question. A `?` inside a bare URL no longer counts as one asked — that published `https://example.com/notes?` |

The operator, on 0.1.44: *"wasm desktop, some terminals i can't scroll up on, unclear why. others i
can. the terminal is running claude. doesn't try to scroll at all, and it has history i can see in
conversation pane (its a fairly long running terminal)"*.

| # | Reading | How | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 387 | **A pane running `claude` keeps no scrollback ring at all — herdr's ring is the main screen's, and Claude Code takes the alternate screen** | A throwaway `kampr-ringprobe` session: `seq 1 400` in a shell pane, `pane.get` for `scroll`, then `claude` in the same pane, `pane.get` again; and `claude` under a bare pty (`research/probe/ptyclient.py`) with its private modes counted | `max_offset_from_bottom` measured **363** after the `seq`, and **0** the moment claude came up — with `pane.read source=recent lines=5000` answering the **39 visible rows** and `truncated: false`, so the 400 lines are unreachable rather than merely unread. The pty says why: claude sets **`?1049h`** at startup. `HerdrProvider::read_scrollback` gates on `scrollback_is_safe_to_read`, so the node's ring for such a pane never grows and the client has nothing above the viewport — the wheel was not broken, there was genuinely nothing there. The transcript is a different source and is why the Conversation view still had the history. Also read off the pty: `?2004h`, `?1004h`, `?2031h`, `?2026h`, and **`?1000h` + `?1006h`** |
| 388 | **Claude Code scrolls its own view on an SGR wheel report, so the notch a terminal would hand to the program actually works** | The same live pane, driven past a screenful (`print the numbers 1 to 120`), then six `\u001b[<64;40;20M` reports through `pane.send_text`, `pane.read visible` before and after | The top row of the screen went from **`90`** to **`85`**. So a wheel forwarded to a pane whose harness holds the whole screen is a real scroll rather than a workaround — and it is what a terminal does with mouse reporting on. **Only measured for claude 2.1.252**: these are pty bytes, and a program that never asked for the mouse has an SGR report typed into it instead, which is why `TAKES_THE_WHEEL` is a table rather than a heuristic |
| 389 | **The hand-off has to be a distance on a phone, not an event** — a finger has no notch, and rounding each frame's travel to whole rows sends nothing on any drag slower than a flick | `PaneScrollTest`, and `PaneWheelTest.aFingerDragPastTheEndOfTheSurfaceScrollsTheProgram` driving the real `TerminalView` with a ten-step touch drag | Four frames of 30 px against a 100 px row is **one** row asked for and 20 px carried, not four frames of nothing. The remainder is per gesture: carried across, the first row of a fresh drag arrives before the finger has travelled it. Direction is the same sense `scrollY` uses — a finger pulled **down** the screen asks for what is above it, which is a wheel-**up** — and the drag hands over only once `scrollBy` has refused, so a pane taller than the window is still Kampr's to pan first |
| 390 | **There is a sane default for every full-screen program, and it is the one herdr already uses: alternate scroll. But it must be the *application* cursor keys — `less`, `man` and `vim` all set DECCKM, and the ordinary `ESC [ B` moves none of them** | `less`, `vim -u NONE`, `vim` and `man ls` under a bare pty with their private modes counted; then each of them live in a throwaway `kampr-altscroll` pane, sent SGR wheel reports, `ESC [ A/B`, `ESC O A/B`, PageDown and ctrl-E, with the top visible row read back each time | **None of the three asks for the mouse** — all set `?1049h` and `?1h`, none sets `?1000h`, so an SGR report is text typed into them. `ESC [ B` x6 moved `less` **not at all**; `ESC O B` x6 moved it **line 1 → line 7**, and `ESC O A` x3 back to **line 4** — a line a press, which is the one-to-one a drag already speaks. `man` the same. In `vim` the cursor moves and the view follows it at the edge (50 presses put line 14 at the top), which is exactly what the operator already sees herdr do at the desk. `vim` also scrolls on **ctrl-E**, and that is the one thing not to send: in insert mode ctrl-E inserts the character below the caret, and nothing outside the pane can see which mode vim is in |
| 391 | **The gate can be general too: herdr reports `foreground_process_group_id == shell_pid` at a prompt and the job's own group otherwise, which the node already folds into `PaneInfo.cmd`** | `pane.process_info` on the same pane at a bash prompt, under `less`, under `vim`, and back at the prompt | At the prompt: `foreground_process_group_id` **652553** = `shell_pid`, one foreground process, `bash`. Under less: group **652801**, name `less`. Under vim: group **652987**, name `vim`. `ProcessInfo::command()` already returns `None` for the first of those, so a null `cmd` on the wire means *the shell may be listening* — and that is the whole gate. It fails closed in both of its meanings: null is also what a pane nothing could read answers, because ble.sh keeps a job in the shell's own group ([#297](#)), and cursor keys into a shell's line editor recall its history. **The shell measured here had ble.sh loaded** (`.bashrc` line 5) and herdr still named the job, so the gate is live on the operator's own machines rather than permanently closed there — [#297](#)'s warning is about what may happen, and the node's procfs walk is the fallback for when it does. So the per-program table shrinks to an **upgrade** list — one row, `claude`, measured to take a real wheel ([#388](#)) — and everything else takes the terminal's default |

The operator, on 0.1.44: *"the fleet run doesn't factor in the .bashrc path / users path, so doing
something like `kampr update` across the fleet doesn't work because ~/.local/bin isn't in the
path"*.

| # | Reading | How | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 392 | **A fleet run was given the service manager's `PATH`, which has neither `~/.local/bin` nor anything else the operator installs into — and said so as a message about a file** | `env -i` shells on the host, then a throwaway example forking a real `Supervisor` twice under a service-manager `PATH`, once with each value | `bash -c` from a clean environment answers **`/usr/local/sbin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin`** and nothing else; `bash -lc` from the *same* clean environment answers **`/home/dbrain/.local/bin:…:/usr/lib/rustup/bin:…`**. So a run forked by the node — a `systemd --user` service — could never find `kampr`, and `Supervisor::spawn` reported it as `No such file or directory (os error 2)`, the same sentence from every host in the herd with nothing in it about a `PATH`. Forking with the login value spawns it. **`$SHELL` was unset in that run**, so the passwd fallback is the rung that answered, which is the case a service manager actually presents. This is [`kampr_herdr::locate`]'s lesson one layer out: a service manager's `PATH` is not the installing shell's |

The operator, on 0.1.44: *"this session's conversation pane is missing messages… it was showing my
previous session's question and outcome up to a certain point, don't think it had the completion
details. when i added a response to this session it showed my response but didn't show your
response to that, but it seems to have updated now"* — on the **mobile** app, on a session that had
been `/clear`ed before use.

| # | Reading | How | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 393 | **`/clear` mints a new session id, opens its transcript immediately, and rewrites the pane's marker in place — all before the next prompt is submitted** | `research/probe/clear/clear.py`: a real claude 2.1.252 under a bare pty, sampling `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` and the project directory at each step | Marker `3be5653f` and no file at start; `3be5653f.jsonl` at 18 KB after one prompt; **at the clear**, the marker reads `858ea4cb` and `858ea4cb.jsonl` already exists at **2142 bytes** while the old file freezes at 18916 and never grows again. Each file carries only its own `sessionId`. So the exact handle the pump keys on — the marker — moves at the clear rather than at the next submit, which is *earlier* than the "created at first submit" rule a fresh session follows ([#311](#)). [#259](#) stands: nothing announces it. **A probe of this has to strip every `CLAUDE_*` variable first** — a claude spawned from inside a claude session inherits `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION`, prints `Transcript saving is off`, and writes neither marker nor transcript, which is why the first two attempts measured nothing at all |
| 394 | **The grid is warm across a re-watch and the conversation is cold — which is why only the transcript went out of date, on the same socket and the same watch** | Read of `session.rs`'s `watch`/`unwatch` against `registry.rs`'s `hold_while`, and of the client's `go`/`watch`/`unwatch` and `Backoff` | `hand_over` calls `registry.hold_while`, which **pins the pane's entry across the stop and start of the session's pump by design ([#252](#))** — so the terminal reattaches to a live emulator, its cell grid and its ring. The conversation is `convo::open()` *inside* the pump the watch created, and `unwatch` is `panes.remove(pane)` then `handle.stop()`, so it is resolve-fold-page from cold every time. Two more readings that bound the problem: switching **terminal ⇄ conversation cancels nothing** (both are one `Screen.Pane`, and `go` unwatches only when the pane id changes), and the socket is **one for the whole app** with a 250 ms → ×1.8 → 15 s ladder that `onForeground` short-circuits outright — so reconnect is not the slow part. The expensive case is a socket that actually dropped: `held` lives on the session, so it dies with it, and the client's turns — which are memory-only, nothing is persisted — are then unconfirmed by anything until a fresh pump pages |

The operator, on 0.1.45: *"when an agent is done done — wasm desktop at least doesn't show
anything. just goes grey. i'd like to see a green flag of some kind when it's done so i know."*

| # | Reading | How | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 395 | **The node destroys herdr's `done` on every pane that has a Claude marker: the marker's `idle` overwrites it, and the operator's green flag had never once reached a client** | The live `0.1.45` node (pid 1427056) and the operator's own `default` herdr, read at the same second three ways: `herdr agent get`/`session.snapshot` for herdr's verdict, `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` for the marker, and the node's own `herd` frame over `ws://127.0.0.1:8790/ws` with a device token. Panes joined to pids through `HERDR_PANE_ID` in `/proc/<pid>/environ`. Control: a throwaway pane given a *reported* `claude` label and driven `working`→`idle` unfocused, so herdr says `done` with **no** marker behind it | Pane `wV:p1` (claude pid 494476): herdr **`done`**, marker **`idle`**, wire **`idle`**. The control pane in the *same herd frame*: herdr **`done`**, no marker, wire **`done`**. `state.rs:627` assigned `harness_status(marker)` unconditionally and that maps `"idle" \| "shell" => Idle`, so `Done` was overwritten three lines before the wire. `AgentStatus::Done` appeared **nowhere** in `crates/kampr-node`; the override (`c3ca477`) landed 17 h after the `done` ordering (`9a52a3e`) and was written without `done` in view. Not an `unwrap_or` but the same failure class — a plausible answer replacing a true one (#233). The whole rest of the pipeline is faithful, and so is the client: `statusOf` maps `"done"` and `HerdPieces` already had `color.done`, `KamprIcons.done`, `MarkShape.Bar` and the word. `kampr-tui`'s `✓` (`sidebar.rs:52`) was unreachable for the same reason. Fixed by `settled_status`, which lets every marker word win **except** `idle`/`shell` over `Done` — the two never disagreed, `done` *is* `idle` plus the half the marker never knew |
| 396 | **`tab.focus` and `workspace.focus` destroy `done` exactly as `pane.focus` does — the rule is "whatever makes the pane the session-focused pane", not one op** | Throwaway session, two workspaces, `pane.report_agent` `working`→`idle` on the unfocused `w2:p1` to arm `done`, then `tab.focus w2:t1`; re-armed, then `workspace.focus w2`; snapshots before and after each, then focus moved back | Both collapse `done` to `idle` and set `focused: true`, and moving focus back does **not** restore it. Kampr's `focus` manage op (`manage.rs:443`) dispatches all three. Supersedes the "only `pane.focus`" half of [#357](#); rule 3 in `AGENTS.md` and `docs/14-agent-status.md` §5 both named one op and now name the rule |
| 397 | **`workspace.create` does not take the session focus**, which is what makes a `done` marker armable in a live test without focusing anything | Two `workspace create` calls on a fresh session, then `workspace list` + `pane list` | The first workspace keeps `focused: true`; the new one is `focused: false`. The harness's own workspace holds the focus, so a pane created for a test is unwatched by construction |
| 398 | **Kampr watching a pane leaves `done` standing and does not stop a later one** | A done-armed pane in the live harness: `watch`, 4 s, `pane.get`; then `working`→`idle` *while watched*; then again after `unwatch` | `done` in all three. The grid stream and its `observe` child are reads, consistent with [#357](#) and with rule 3's "every read leaves it standing" |
| 399 | **`done` was observed standing on the session-*focused* pane, which [#357](#)'s focused case does not predict — and the mechanism is not established** | Read-only `session.snapshot` against the operator's live `default` session with a herdr client attached (pid 1350686), while the operator was working in a browser | `focused_workspace_id: "wV"`, `focused_pane_id: "wV:p1"`, that pane `focused: true` **and** `agent_status: "done"`. A throwaway reproduction of the same transition on a focused pane gives `idle`, so focus-at-the-desk is not the whole test. **Guess, and recorded as one:** herdr may consume terminal focus-in/focus-out from the host emulator and count every pane as unwatched while the terminal window itself is unfocused — which would make `done` sound for an operator reading Kampr in a browser. Establishing it needs a focus event written to a live desk's client tty and was not done. A second unresolved edge from the same work: a pane reported `blocked` before the `working`→`idle` edge did not always get a `done`, and re-asserting `working` immediately before the `idle` report is reliable where it was not |

The operator, on 0.1.45: *"when i launch claude and notice the screen is small on wasm desktop i use
the zoom panel's 'match this view' — it works but it doesn't horizontally update until a message
goes through, so typing a prompt ends up typing off the visible cols."*

| # | Reading | How | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 400 | **A controller resize reflows the screen already on the pane, so the rows re-wrap at the new grid with no new output — while the layout rect never moves** | Headless `herdr server --session kampr-probe-widthadopt`, workspace over the API; `exec /bin/sh`, `PS1='$ '`, then `clear; printf '%.0s#' $(seq 1 400); echo`; `pane.read` at `source: recent` and `recent_unwrapped`, `lines: 40`, `format: text`; then `herdr terminal session control w1:p1 --cols 120 --rows 40`, killed after 3 s, and the same two reads plus `session.snapshot` | Before: physical `[93, 93, 93, 93, 28]` against one logical line of **400** — a PTY of 93 on a rect of 94, the scrollbar column of [#230](#). After the controller had gone and **with nothing printed**, the same line came back as `[120, 120, 120, 40]` and `rect.width` still read **94**. So herdr re-lays-out the pane's existing screen at the new grid and `ARCHITECTURE.md` §4.2's walk re-proves itself from content that was already there — which is exactly why a *shell* pane appears to handle `pane.size` correctly and hides the defect below it |
| 401 | **A pane with nothing wrapped on it hands the width walk nothing at all, so a resize stays invisible until something wraps** | The same pane with `clear` typed, then the same two reads; then, against a real node, `pane.size` to 117 columns on that cleared pane with a client watching | `recent` is `"$\n"` and `recent_unwrapped` is `"$"` — one short row, one short line, no join. The reading is `wrapped: None`, so a standing proof only *ages* ([#211](#)) and the stream keeps the width proved before the resize; past the proof lifetime it falls back to the rect, which [#68](#) established is fiction. Against the node: **not one `grid.reset` in 30 s**. This is the shape of every full-screen agent's screen and it is the operator's report. Fixed by `HerdrProvider::resized`: `pane.size` is the one width the node did not infer, so it is adopted as a proof and wakes every running probe at once — recorded only in `hold` mode (a held controller *is* the geometry, [#18](#)) or when the `once` mode's `viewport_rows` check says it stuck, because an attached desk takes the geometry straight back ([#19](#)) and a width the PTY does not have would crop every client. **A test that leaves a wrapped line on the screen passes with the defect present** |

| # | Reading | How | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 402 | **Claude Code 2.1.252 writes a launched agent's transcript *after* the `toolUseResult` that names it, and `settle` runs once with no retry — so a poll in that window lost the card for the life of the session** | Two `run_in_background: true` launches in throwaway `claude -p` sessions, a 50 ms `os.walk` over the project directory recording first-sight of every file, read against the record timestamps in the transcript itself | The result record carrying `{"isAsync":true,"status":"async_launched","agentId":…}` was written **+0.012 s** and **+0.585 s** after its own `tool_use` — at launch — with completion arriving separately **+116.7 s** later as a `queue-operation`. But the subagent `.jsonl` hit the disk **+0.101 s** and **+0.777 s** *after* that result. `ClaudeParser::settle` runs once per `tool_use_id` and `subagent::launched` returned `None` while `transcript.is_file()` was false, so the `Block::Sub` was dropped and never retried. **176 of 177 launches in the operator's archive are `async_launched`**, so this was the common path. Fixed by naming the file instead of finding it: `Registry::open_sub` resolves through `TranscriptRoot::contain`, which canonicalises, so it refuses until the file exists and succeeds the moment it appears — a transcript 100 ms late and one that is never coming are the same question, and open time is where it can be answered honestly |
| 403 | **`agent-<id>.meta.json` is written at launch, ~15 ms after the `tool_use` record, and its `toolUseId` is the only link a running *synchronous* `Agent` call has to the agent it started** | `stat -c %.9Y` on the meta against the launching record's timestamp for four synchronous launches across two sessions; then `stat -c %W` on all 234 metas in `~/.claude/projects/*/*/subagents/` against the `tool_use` that started each; inode/size/mtime/ctime sampled at 50 ms across one background agent's whole life | **+0.012, +0.018, +0.020, +0.020 s** after the call — 85–146 s ahead of the result, because a synchronous launch (`run_in_background: false`, new in 2.1.252) writes no `toolUseResult` for that long and its input carries `{description, subagent_type, run_in_background, prompt}` with **no `agentId`**. The subagent `.jsonl` is there at the same instant and **grows the whole way** (8 595 → 25 842 bytes over 85 s in ten steps). Three concurrent launches each got a meta with its **own distinct `toolUseId`**, so matching is by id and a creation-order heuristic is neither needed nor defensible. Over the archive **150 of 176** timeable metas were born within 1 s of their call. The meta is **never rewritten** (inode, size, mtime and ctime unchanged for 104 s through a `SendMessage` and through completion), so the **26 of 176 (15%)** born 229 s – 11 030 s late are genuinely late files — and **all 26 are `async_launched`**, where the meta is not needed. No mechanism found for the tail; it costs nothing while the meta only supplies the `Explore — <title>` label and `agentId` comes from the result |

| # | Reading | How | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 404 | **[#270](#)'s one-column filter is what left the most common symbol in the corpus rendering as tofu, and two of the four gaps were Kampr's own chrome** | Re-ran #270's corpus method over `~/.claude/projects`, `~/.codex/sessions` and `~/.gemini` (320 files, 775M characters) with the one-column filter **removed**, subtracted each shipped face's Skia coverage, and counted distinct files as well as occurrences to separate a real screen character from a one-file symbol dump | `U+2705 ✅` is the **most common missing codepoint anywhere in the corpus** — 504 occurrences across 42 files — and `U+274C ❌` second at 134/24. Both were absent from **all four** faces; the operator screenshotted a `Size \| Path \| Swept?` table in a wasm pane with five tofu boxes and one ASCII `X`. #270 could not have found them: its filter kept only glyphs "exactly one column wide", on the belief that a wide glyph goes down the fallback path [#214](#) built. **It does not.** #214 built a way to *position* a wide glyph across the two cells it owns; nothing *supplies* one, and in a browser there is nothing behind Skia at all. The face already carried 39 wide emoji inherited from JetBrains Mono, all at advance 600, so nothing new is asked of the grid. Two further gaps were **Kampr's own**: `U+2691 ⚑` (206/23) is the blocked-agent flag `kampr-tui` draws at `sidebar.rs:50`, tofu in every browser pane showing a `kampr` session, and `U+21F1 ⇱` (103/16) is herdr's fit indicator. **No donor on any build machine carries ✅ or ❌** — Noto Sans Symbols 2 stops short of the emoji dingbats and Noto Color Emoji is CBDT bitmaps with no outline — so both are **aliased** onto `U+2713`/`U+2717` per [#271](#), which is also the only path that keeps the weight. Faces went 2220 → **2224** codepoints, **0 lost, 0 advances moved**, vertical metrics untouched, `--verify` still reproducing 861/861 cut-ins |

The `0.1.46` CI run went red on two live tests that pass on the operator's machine, both at
`herdr_says` — the instrument that proves the state under test was armed, not the claim.

| # | Reading | How | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 405 | **A `pane.report_agent` arms herdr's `done` only once herdr's *own* detection has published about the pane: a `working` report inside the post-label `unknown` hold arms it for ~0.4 s, and nothing re-arms it afterwards** | `research/probe/done-arming.py`, three modes against fresh headless `herdr server --session kampr-probe-donearm-*` sessions: two `workspace create`s so the pane under test is the unfocused one ([#397](#)), a copy of `sleep` named `claude` run in it, then `working`, a measured gap, `idle`, and `pane.get` polled for `done`. Swept the gap (0–20 s), the *anchor* of the first report (straight after `pane.process_info` shows the process, versus after `pane.get` first says `idle` with `agent: "claude"` by itself), the binary's name, a re-report of `working` in front of the `idle`, and reads-versus-pure-sleep inside the gap | Anchored inside the hold: `done` at 0, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3 and 0.4 s — **`idle` at 0.45 s and at every gap above it tried (0.45, 0.5, 0.6, 0.8, 1.2, 2, 4, 8 s)**, with `pane.get` reading `working` the whole way, because the report still outranks the fallback ([#358](#)). Anchored past herdr's own first publish — which lands at **3.97–4.02 s** after the command is sent, n=10, the 3.33 s `unknown` hold of [#360](#) plus the label tick — `done` in **10/10** runs at gaps of 0, 0.5, 2, 5 and 10 s, and it then **stands for the 20 s each run watched it**. So the window is herdr's own detection converging on `default_known_agent_idle_fallback` and taking the pane's transition history with it, not a report TTL: a pane herdr never labelled — the same `sleep` named `nap`, or none started at all, with the agent invented by the report ([#358](#)) — arms `done` at 2 s and at 20 s alike. **Re-reporting `working` immediately in front of the `idle` does not re-arm** (2/2 `idle`), and reads in the gap change nothing ([#357](#)) — a pure sleep behaves identically. This is the CI failure: both tests reported into the hold, and a loaded runner spends more than 0.4 s between two socket calls where this machine spends 2 ms. Fixed by `herdr_has_scraped` in `live.rs`, a read that waits for herdr's own first answer before anything is reported into the pane; with it both tests survive a **deliberate 2 s** gap between the reports, and without it `a_pane_that_finished_unwatched_stays_done_when_its_harness_says_idle` fails on this machine with CI's exact words. Resolves the second half of [#399](#) — a `blocked` opener never mattered, an early report did; the focused-pane `done` in its first half is still unexplained |
